'src/**/*.ts'\r\n  - 'app/**/*.{ts,vue}'\r\n  - 'tests/**/*.ts'\r\n  - 'configs/**/*.ts'\r\n  - 'vite.config.ts'\r\n  - 'tsconfig.json'\r\n  - 'package.json'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Architecture and placement rules\r\n\r\n## Centralized-file pattern\r\n\r\n| Content                   | Sole location                                                |\r\n| ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |\r\n| Interfaces/types          | `*/types.ts`                                                 |\r\n| Constants/data            | `*/constants.ts`                                             |\r\n| Template definitions      | `*/templates.ts`                                             |\r\n| Pure helpers              | `*/helpers.ts`                                               |\r\n| Guards                    | `*/validators.ts`                                            |\r\n| Guard combinators         | `*/combinators.ts`                                           |\r\n| Owned snapshots           | `*/cloners.ts`                                               |\r\n| Coercers                  | `*/parsers.ts`                                               |\r\n| Shape values              | `*/shapers.ts`                                               |\r\n| Value inferers            | `*/inferers.ts`                                              |\r\n| Shape/algorithm compilers | `*/compilers.ts`                                             |\r\n| Entity/value factories    | `*/factories.ts`                                             |\r\n| Middleware factories      | `*/middlewares.ts`                                           |\r\n| Request handlers          | `*/handlers.ts`                                              |\r\n| Route tables              | `*/routes.ts`                                                |\r\n| Seeders                   | `*/seeders.ts`                                               |\r\n| Schemas                   | `*/schemas.ts`                                               |\r\n| Compiled contracts        | `*/contracts.ts`                                             |\r\n| Relations                 | `*/relations.ts`                                             |\r\n| Error classes/guards      | `*/errors.ts`                                                |\r\n| Public exports            | `*/index.ts`                                                 |\r\n| Implementations           | `*/[domain]/[Entity].ts` - one class per file                |\r\n| Function modules          | a designated folder's `[function].ts`, one function per file |\r\n\r\nUse only the centralized files an environment needs.\r\n\r\n## Declaration placement\r\n\r\n- An implementation file contains imports and exactly one class implementation with `#` fields.\r\n- It contains no module-scope interface, type, constant, or free function-even when private to that file.\r\n- A function module contains imports and exactly one exported function named for its file. Everything else extracts by kind exactly as it does from a class module.\r\n- Extract local declarations by kind. \"Only used here\" and \"not exported\" are not exemptions.\r\n- Every declaration in a centralized file is exported. Fold away a trivial single-use declaration or export/test it; never leave it hidden.\r\n- The only permitted non-exported module-scope declarations are in a runtime entrypoint that must be self-contained and cannot import siblings, such as raw source loaded in a worker. Explain that necessity in a comment.\r\n- A runtime entry-`src/bin/main.ts`, `app/browser/main.ts`, `app/server/main.ts`-is a fixed name, not a centralized kind file. Both the data rule and the function rule reach it, so it declares no module-scope constant and no module-scope function: it imports what it needs and runs. The preceding self-contained exception covers only an entrypoint that cannot import siblings.\r\n- Perform a cleanup pass after implementation: no stray implementation-file declarations, non-exported/wrong-kind centralized declarations, prohibited nested declarations, duplicate implementations, compatibility aliases, superfluous wrappers, stale imports/barrel rows, or untested extracted functions.\r\n\r\n## Kind purity\r\n\r\n- Each centralized file contains only its named kind.\r\n- Module-scope constants live only in `constants.ts`, use UPPER_SNAKE_CASE, and freeze object/array data with `Object.freeze`.\r\n- A camelCase namespace containing functions is helper behavior, not constant data; place it in `helpers.ts`.\r\n- `helpers.ts` is exported reusable infrastructure. A reusable regex, parser, header flattener, signer, or similar fragment has one implementation used by source, tests, and fixtures.\r\n- A would-be helper has these outcomes:\r\n  - trivial and genuinely single-use \u001a fold into its caller;\r\n  - non-trivial or reusable \u001a extract, export, unit-test, and route every duplicate through it.\r\n- `factories.ts`, `compilers.ts`, and `parsers.ts` are centralized files, not hiding places. Factory glue extracts to `helpers.ts`; pure compiler/parser recursion remains exported in its own kind file.\r\n- Every exported function in `parsers.ts` is named `parse*`. Every exported function in\r\n- No role commits, pushes, tags, publishes, installs dependencies, or runs a destructive command.\r\n- No role runs `git checkout`, `git restore`, `git stash`, `git reset`, or `git clean`. Each discards\r\n  a working-tree change silently. A role that must undo its own edit undoes exactly that edit.\r\n- A dispatch that has a unit plant a line to prove an instrument can fail names a file the unit under\r\n  verification did not touch, and names how the plant is removed. Check the tree's status before\r\n  choosing the file.\r\n- No role reads, prints, copies, uploads, or packages a secret - `CURSOR_API_KEY`, Codex auth\r\n  files, `.env*`, `.npmrc`, `auth.json`, keys, or tokens.\r\n- Concurrent executors never run tree-wide `format`, lint `--fix`, or `build`. They validate\r\n  read-only and scoped to their own files.\r\n- Keep hooks light. A Stop hook may run cheap changed-file verification such as `git diff --check`.\r\n  It never duplicates the gate suite; gates belong to `verifier`.\r\n\r\n## Context and decomposition\r\n\r\n- Keep the main context at decision level. Send large reads, repository scans, raw logs, diffs,\r\n  and exploratory sweeps to `grok` and consume the distillate.\r\n- Settle a behavioural question by running it, including your own. This binds before a brief is\r\n  written, not only after a report arrives, and it needs no disagreement to trigger it. A round of\r\n  deliberation that a probe would have ended is the expensive failure, and it is invisible because\r\n  it feels like rigour.\r\n- Check an assumption before it enters the plan. An unverified belief the Orchestrator states\r\n  becomes a fact for every unit downstream, and every one of them inherits the error.\r\n- Break a repeating frame deliberately. When several rounds against one subject keep finding the\r\n  same class of defect through a new door, the search is following the frame rather than the\r\n  defect. Bound the scope, then fan out independent lenses over disjoint slices in one pass.\r\n  Parallelism is worth more here for the framing it breaks than for the wall-clock it saves.\r\n- When the recurring class has a direction - each fix relocates it along one stream: a dependency\r\n  chain, a data path, a call chain - the source sits elsewhere on that stream, and deepening the\r\n  current station cannot reach it. Switch from depth to breadth: fan probes over the stream's\r\n  stations in parallel, blind and clean-contexted, as far up and down as the stream runs, to locate\r\n  the source. Then plan downstream from the source with the sweep's map of how far the defect\r\n  reaches, so the remaining work carries a measured bound instead of an open count of rounds. The\r\n  seam budget in `.claude/rules/quality.md` \u0015 Rounds and verdicts states when this fires.\r\n- The subjective and objective lanes are the adversarial pass's FLOOR, not its shape. Where a\r\n  subject has more seams than that pass can attack, fan out one lens per seam over disjoint slices,\r\n  keep every lens blind and\r\n  clean-contexted, and number every slice's claims in one shared sequence. Change the lenses in a\r\n  successor round rather than repeating them.\r\n- Decompose by required context and independently verifiable acceptance criteria, not by task type.\r\n- Send instructions down fully specified. Return findings smaller than the context consumed.\r\n- Parallelize independent work. Serialize dependencies and shared-file contention.\r\n- The Orchestrator owns the plan and every final decision. Design engines propose, writers execute,\r\n  auditors advise.\r\n\r\n## Writing concurrency\r\n\r\nConcurrent executors share a filesystem unless isolated. Follow these rules to prevent clobbered\r\nedits, formatter and build races, cache phantoms, and validation cross-talk.\r\n\r\n1. Serialize writers as \u0015 Permission floor states: one per checkout, checkouts disjoint. Commit a\r\n   checkpoint before each writing dispatch so git is the rollback mechanism.\r\n2. Assign disjoint owned files plus explicit shared and off-limits files.\r\n3. Keep shared files report-only. Executors return exact patches for serial integration.\r\n4. Restrict concurrent executors to read-only, scoped validation. A tree-wide result may contain a\r\n   sibling's in-flight failure, so an executor reports only its owned scope.\r\n5. Give concurrent audit lanes worktree isolation whenever the campaign is uncommitted. Lanes\r\n   sharing one working tree contaminate each other's readings in both directions.\r\n6. After integration, clear shared caches if needed, then have one independent `verifier` run the\r\n   authoritative tree-wide sweep. A writer's self-report never establishes green.\r\n7. The Orchestrator's own sweep is a writing dispatch and queues behind the units that own those\r\n   files. A script that fixes one thing across every target is the most direct way to break the\r\n   serialization rule, because it does not feel like a dispatch - nobody was named, no brief was\r\n   written, and it finishes in seconds. It still writes into trees a live unit owns, and a unit\r\n   whose brief it invalidates will repair the same drift the other way and report a state that is\r\n   already false. Run it before the units, or after them, or send the decision to every unit in\r\n   flight per the mid-campaign rule under **Dispatch anatomy**. Never beside them.\r\n8. A fleet pass that records a per-target status commits only the targets it recorded green. Reading\r\n   \"is the tree dirty\" instead of \"did this target pass\" pushes a red target the moment one exists,\r\n   and a flake makes that look like it worked. Refuse the failed row, name it, and re-run it alone\r\n   before deciding what it was.\r\n9. Run a fleet pass in slices that report as they finish, never as one block. A block hides its first\r\n   failure behind every target that follows, so the failure surfaces after the work it exists to\r\n   stop. A slice hands control back while most of the fleet is still unstarted.\r\n10. Re-run a timing or resource failure alone before believing it. Concurrent slices, builds, and\r\n    suites make a container miss deadlines it meets when idle, so a red result under load is a\r\n    question rather than an answer. A unit re-running the file alone is not alone: its own exec,\r\n    code-mode host, and sandbox stay resident throughout, and on a small container that residue\r\n    alone misses a deadline the same file meets on an idle one. So the deciding re-run belongs to\r\n    the Orchestrator after the unit exits, never to the unit, and a timing failure a unit cannot\r\n    clear is carried to that reading rather than diagnosed by the unit. This makes a writer's own\r\n    gate evidence systematically pessimistic on timing, which is another reason the independent\r\n    `verifier` runs the authoritative gates.\r\n11. While any unit is live, the Orchestrator's own instruments go in its scratchpad, never in the\r\n    subject repository's `tmp/`. A probe that both writes and deletes inside the subject tree can\r\n    remove a file it did not create, and a cleanup keyed to a caller-supplied path list is how. The\r\n    same directory is where dispatched units build their instruments, so removing it destroys a live\r\n    lane's work.\r\n\r\n## Execution loop\r\n\r\nAt session start, before planning, probe bench liveness and plan routing against the result. Resolve\r\neach CLI first (`codex --version`; `agent --version`, falling back to `agent.cmd --version`), then\r\nrun the bench's authentication-state check where it exposes one. Neither answer is liveness. A\r\nversion string proves the binary is installed, and an authentication-state check reads stored\r\ncredentials, so both pass while the account is out of quota, while the routed model is unavailable to\r\nit, while the server has already revoked the credential the check read, and inside a sandbox\r\nwith the network denied. Record a bench live only on a bounded round-tripped model call that came\r\nback, and record what came back beside the routing decision. Probes are read-only, and the role file\r\nowns each bench's exact probe.\r\n\r\nThe local steps still run, because they route the recovery rather than decide the verdict: an\r\nunresolved CLI is an install problem, a failed authentication-state check starts the login ladder\r\nin Recovering a dark bench, and a bench that passes both and still cannot round-trip is dark for a\r\nreason no local check can see. Record every dark bench with its fallback and the lane substitution\r\nit forces, and never absorb one silently. A readiness script reports readiness and performs no\r\nmodel call, so the round trip belongs to the Orchestrator's own probe or to the bridge carrying\r\nthe unit, never to the hook. Liveness also expires: a dispatch that fails on quota, model access,\r\nor the network is a fresh liveness result rather than a unit-level fault, so record the bench dark\r\nfrom there and re-plan the lane instead of re-dispatching against a session-start answer that no\r\nlonger holds.\r\n\r\n1. **Absorb.** Dispatch `grok` for terrain, prior art, and the reading the decision needs. In an\r\n   Orkestrel repo dispatch `orkestrel` alongside it for live package state. Skip only when the\r\n   ground is already known.\r\n2. **Design adversarially.** Run the adversarial pass on one design brief: `planner` for\r\n   the subjective lane and `analyst` for the objective lane. Reconcile them yourself into one plan:\r\n   units, dependencies, ownership, parallel and serial order, acceptance criteria, risks.\r\n   - Surface the plan before dispatch, including a routing ledger naming each unit's role **and**\r\n     engine. Routing a unit to a Claude-native agent when its work class belongs to a bench -\r\n     reading-heavy to Grok, objective audit or objective implementation to Sol - without a recorded\r\n     bench-dark deviation is a dispatch deviation.\r\n   - State the goal's exit criterion beside the units: the enumerated capabilities whose closure\r\n     ends the campaign, each to end implemented, repaired, retained, or intentionally excluded on\r\n     evidence. A plan that names work but not its end can only be abandoned, never finished.\r\n3. **Implement.** Route each nontrivial objective unit to the Sol `implementer` and each nontrivial\r\n   subjective unit to the Opus `implementer`, in the checkout the unit writes, one writer per\r\n   checkout. Route a fully specified taste-free unit to `builder`. Never route implementation to an\r\n   engine the unit's judgment load exceeds.\r\n4. **Integrate.** Evaluate each distillate against its acceptance criteria, apply shared-file\r\n   patches serially, and route cross-cutting findings. Integration applies exact returned patches\r\n   and mechanical conflict resolution only. A new type, mechanism, behavior, or acceptance\r\n   criterion discovered at integration is a successor brief routed to a writer, never an\r\n   integration edit.\r\n5. **Audit adversarially.** Audit every nontrivial implementation with the objective lane and the\r\n   subjective lane - `analyst` and `reviewer`, the way the design step names its lanes - at least\r\n   one of them on an engine that did not write the work. Dispatch `checker` in addition when the\r\n   acceptance criteria are mechanical - counts, paths, parity rows, scope honesty - never in place\r\n   of a lane. A round that runs fewer lanes than its brief names, or omits the checker its criteria\r\n   call for, records the deviation in its verdict file with that round's own reason, never a\r\n   template sentence.\r\n   - State the audit's subject as numbered falsifiable claims and require per-claim verdicts with\r\n     evidence, per the Falsification law in `.claude/rules/quality.md` and the `orkestrel-falsify`\r\n     value set, unless the dispatch names a different skill that fixes another.\r\n   - In a fix round, give the unit to an auditor engine that did not write it.\r\n   - Run the `orkestrel-falsify` skill for multi-round audits. Strike a unit whose subject a later unit deletes. State the new\r\n     prerequisite of a unit that acquired one.\r\n   - Walk the remaining units once and ask of each whether what landed still supports it. A\r\n     decision taken inside a unit can remove a later unit's foundation, and the unit that took it\r\n     cannot see that.\r\n   - Re-baseline when a probe overturns a decision the plan rests on, not only at a phase boundary.\r\n     A measurement that falsifies your own reconciliation changes which units run.\r\n   - Record what changed and why. A\r\nre-baseline changes which units run. It never changes the goal's exit criterion.\r\n\r\nStrike a unit because the phase satisfied it, never because it became inconvenient. Add a unit\r\nbecause implementation revealed work the exit criterion already required, never because an engine\r\nthought of something else worth doing. A re-baseline that moves the exit criterion is a rescope, and\r\nthat needs the user.\r\n\r\n## Deviation protocol\r\n\r\nWhen reality diverges from a writing dispatch:\r\n\r\n1. The writer stops and reports: expected, found, exact evidence, done or not done, and at most one\r\n   short hypothesis. It does not investigate, improvise, or alter the plan.\r\n2. The Orchestrator triages:\r\n   - obvious correction \u001a tighten and re-dispatch;\r\n   - missing mechanical evidence \u001a dispatch `verifier`;\r\n   - unknown terrain \u001a dispatch `grok` with the report and the plan slice;\r\n   - unknown design or root cause \u001a dispatch `planner` and `analyst` on the question.\r\n3. The Orchestrator decides, updates the plan, and re-dispatches.\r\n\r\nWorkflow failures use the same ladder. Do not absorb their raw logs into the main context.\r\n\r\n## Dispatch anatomy\r\n\r\n### Native first\r\n\r\nLaunch a model through the running harness's own mechanism whenever that harness hosts it: Claude\r\nsubagents and workflows in Claude Code, Codex-native agents in a Codex session, Cursor-native\r\nsessions in Cursor. A clean pass on its own\r\n  engine's work is the least valuable result a lane can return.\r\n- Supply the evidence the subject type requires, per the table above.\r\n- Auditors edit no source and spawn nothing. Exactly one of these values:\r\n\r\n   | value           | meaning                                                                                | evidence                                                                                                                                                      |\r\n   | --------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n   | `CONFIRMED`     | attacked and it held                                                                   | as the Falsification law requires                                                                                                                             |\r\n   | `BROKEN`        | falsified                                                                              | as the Falsification law requires - note it says _input, **state, or interleaving**_, so a concurrency claim is falsified by an interleaving, not by an input |\r\n   | `UNRESOLVED`    | cannot be decided from the evidence available                                          | what would settle it; Neither accepts; the orchestrator does. This is where a round becomes a\r\ndecision, and it is not delegable.\r\n\r\n## Reproduce before you act\r\n\r\nThe rule beneath this whole section: **run it rather than argue it.** Every judgement below is\r\ncheap once the probe exists and unreliable until it does.\r\n\r\nAn auditor's finding is a **hypothesis** until the orchestrator has run it. Reproduce every sharp\r\nclaim by hand, against the built output, before it enters a fix brief.\r\n\r\n**Build the hostile input outside the `try`.** A probe that wraps construction and invocation in one\r\ncatch cannot distinguish _the subject threw_ from _my harness threw_ - a missing import, a wrong\r\narity, a `require` in an ESM context all surface as the finding you were hoping to see. Construct\r\nfirst, let harness failures crash loudly, and only guard the call under test.\r\n\r\nReproduction produces these outcomes, and all of them matter:\r\n\r\n- the finding **confirms** and is often **wider** than reported - the reproduction reaches doors the\r\n  auditor did not try;\r\n- the finding **confirms but is bounded smaller** - real, and not where the auditor thought;\r\n- the finding **evaporates**, because the auditor's input could not exercise what it claimed to test.\r\n\r\nSeparate a dead finding from a dead vector before evaporating anything. A reported vector the\r\ncompiler rejects refutes the vector alone; re-derive one the types admit, and record which vector\r\nwas actually tested.\r\n\r\nThe same reproduction discipline applies to your own probes. A probe whose input cannot reach the\r\ncode under test reports a pass that means nothing, and it will read exactly like a real pass.\r\n\r\n## A disagreement is rarely a tie\r\n\r\nWhen auditors return opposite verdicts on one claim, do not average them and do not prefer the\r\nengine you trust more. **Reproduce first** - running the disagreement settles most of them outright,\r\nand it is the only method that can also find what neither auditor saw. Then find the question each\r\none answered. The common shapes:\r\n\r\n- **Both right about different objects.** One tested a case the other did not construct. This is a\r\n  `SPLIT-CLAIM`: the claim was a universal that carried more than one subject. It is **not** a\r\n  further verdict value - one falsifying input makes a universal claim `BROKEN`, and succeeding on a\r\n  different object does not undo that. Split it, keep the original `BROKEN` if any subclaim is\r\n  broken, and carry the split into the successor brief.\r\n- **Both right about different halves of one claim number.** Same resolution: the claim number was\r\n  carrying more than one claim. Split and renumber.\r\n- **One right on the mechanism, the other on the criterion.** Take both. The reconciled ruling is\r\n  frequently neither proposal, and better than either, because each supplied a constraint the other\r\n  violated.\r\n- **The consequence disproves the premise.** An argument that an input class is unreachable is\r\n  answered by running it and showing what the reachable consequence is.\r\n\r\nRecord which engine was right and on what. A round whose disagreements are smoothed over teaches\r\nnothing to the next one.\r\n\r\n## Evidence custody\r\n\r\nBlind reports are immutable, and their independence is a property of the record, not of anyone's\r\nmemory. A reader six months out must be able to tell an unbiased blind verdict from one produced\r\nafter an auditor saw its counterpart's evidence - otherwise the whole value of running blind is\r\nunverifiable after the fact.\r\n\r\nThese rules are enforceable:\r\n\r\n1. **A returned verdict is never edited** - not by the auditor, not by the orchestrator.\r\n2. **Anything an auditor says after seeing another's report is a separate file beside that verdict**,\r\n   under `.orkestrel/<package>/`, named for the unit and the exposure, recording what was shown and\r\n   to whom. It is a durable record, not a journal, so it survives the campaign sweep.\r\n\r\nThat exchange is a **fallback, not a phase.** Reproduction comes first and settles most\r\ndisagreements. Reach for an exchange only when a specific factual question survives reproduction,\r\nscope it to that question, initiate it yourself, and run it once - a second exchange is negotiation.\r\n\r\n**Ask the auditor to attack the other's evidence on that question. Never ask it to resolve the\r\ndisagreement, reconsider its position, or say whether the other changed its mind.** Constraining\r\nwhen, who, scope and frequency does nothing about convergence if the instruction itself invites it,\r\nand \"does their evidence change your claim?\" is the convergence prompt in its purest form.\r\n\r\n## Bound the finding\r\n\r\nState what is **not** broken, and why the adjacent behaviour that looks identical is correct. A\r\nfinding without a boundary is an alarm, and alarms get discounted wholesale - including the true\r\nones next to them.\r\n\r\nThese boundaries earn their keep:\r\n\r\n- **Credit what the round got right.** If the hostile inputs adjacent to the hole are correctly\r\n  contained, say so and list them. It sharpens the finding to a point instead of an area.\r\n- **Show where the same-looking answer is correct.** When several exports answer a hostile input the\r\n  same way and only one is wrong, name what makes the difference - usually that the correct ones\r\n  agree with a documented view, and the wrong one reads on an axis it then ignores.\r\n\r\n## Bound the fix before briefing it\r\n\r\nEstablish what over-correcting would break, and put it in the fix brief as a constraint. Both ends\r\nare usually wrong:\r\n\r\n- **too little** - a patch to the one function, leaving the package holding conflicting standards\r\n  for the same thing, which is the inconsistency that produced the finding;\r\n- **too much** - adopting the strictest sibling's rule verbatim, breaking a legitimate caller\r\n  pattern, and tripping \"no refusal was widened into a regression\" in the next round.\r\n\r\nFind the rule that fits both. It is usually about **agreement** rather than about categories - what\r\na reader reads, its answer must carry - and it dissolves the special cases rather than enumerating\r\nthem.\r\n\r\nMeasure a proposed fix before adopting it; it is itself a claim. Run it against the set it must\r\nnot break, including every case an earlier round pinned. Where it fails that set, document the\r\nlimit on the helper that owns it and pin the limit with a test that names it as one. A heuristic\r\nthat trades one wrong answer for another fails quietly; a stated boundary does not.\r\n\r\nWhere the choice is genuinely open, it is a design judgement with a subjective and an objective\r\nhalf, and it goes to a blind design pass before code. Ruling it unilaterally is how a fix round\r\nbecomes the next audit's finding.\r\n\r\n## Certifying an instrument\r\n\r\nThe control-population law in `.claude/rules/quality.md` binds here without restatement. What it\r\nleaves this round is the procedure.\r\n\r\nWhen a round certifies an instrument - a pin, an identity check, a generated sweep - the controls\r\nare usually drawn from whatever the instrument obviously covers, because that is where the examples\r\ntake the least construction. That sampling proves discrimination _within_ the population and is\r\nroutinely reported as proof the instrument works.\r\n\r\nSo before running controls, write down the instrument's **membership rule** in one sentence, then\r\nask what the rule excludes. Draw at least one control from there. These shapes have already cost a\r\nround each:\r\n\r\n- an AST comparison whose controls were all drawn from the literal classes present in the bodies it\r\n  guarded, blind to the classes absent from them;\r\n- a call-closure pin whose controls were all body-reachable functions, green for a function reached\r\n  only through a parameter default.\r\n\r\nThen write the sentences that matter: what the controls established, and what they did not. What\r\nthey did not establish is the one that gets skipped, and skipping it is how an instrument's\r\ncredibility outruns its evidence.\r\n\r\n## Ruling\r\n\r\n- Every retained finding names the fix-brief item that carries it. A finding with no carrier is a\r\n  dropped finding; walk the list once and check.\r\n- Drop, **on the record**, anything neither auditor can substantiate against the evidence.\r\n- Promote anything that must outlive the round into a durable artifact before the working files are\r\n  swept. What lives only in a scratch file did not survive.\r\n- The fix round's auditor is an engine that did not write it.\r\n- The next round's brief is this round's successor.\r\n\r\n## The threshold\r\n\r\nAccept when the brief's claims are **satisfied on evidence** - the `PASS` terminal line the skill\r\ndefines, against a claim set that covers what the subject owns. Not green gates.\r\n\r\nA round that finds something is the process working. A round that finds nothing because nobody tried\r\nis the failure; a round re-run because an attack can still be imagined never ends. Bound the claim\r\nset at the brief, rule on what it returned, and close.\r\nexport * from './types.js'\r\nexport * from './constants.js'\r\nexport * from './errors.js'\r\nexport * from The body it sends is an owned snapshot of that projection, so a value's own `toJSON` method is ignored rather than consulted and cannot reach the wire. A `ToolCall.caller` is local context and never crosses the hop.\n1007:35. **The wire shapes and the compiled contracts.** `toolCallShape`, `messageShape`, `providerRequestShape`, `providerResultShape`, and `relayFrameShape` declare each domain type's JSON projection, and `messageContract`, `providerRequestContract`, `providerResultContract`, and `relayFrameContract` compile them into guards and parsers. Every projection is strictly narrower than the domain type it mirrors, and the narrowing is the point: `ToolCall.arguments` is `Record<string, unknown>` in the domain and a JSON record on the wire, so a function-valued argument is refused rather than dropped silently, and a tool's `parameters` and a call's `schema` are refused the same way. `ToolCall.caller` is local context and appears in no shape — the guard refuses a record carrying it and the parser drops it, so it cannot cross a hop. `relayFrameShape` is a channel-discriminated union that admits no member outside the arm it matched, so a decorative field on an `error` frame is a refusal rather than a tolerated extra. The round trip is closed in both directions: the request `RelayProvider.body` projects parses back to the value the relay hands upstream, and the frame `RelayStream` writes parses back to the delta or the result the browser decodes. **The wire body is an owned snapshot.** `body` clones the projection through Contract's `cloneJSONValue` and validates that clone, so the bytes `JSON.stringify` produces are exactly what the guard saw. The clone reads property descriptors rather than accessors, so a synthetic `toJSON` method on a call's `arguments`, a tool's `parameters`, or the options `schema` is ignored rather than consulted and reaches no wire — the snapshot is the sole mechanism, and the refusal that remains covers a value outside JSON, a hostile read, and a snapshot the contract rejects. That refusal carries the clone's own failure as its `cause`. A caller mutating its own objects after the call cannot change what was sent.\n1083:### Relaying a browser provider through your own server\n1094:import type { DispatcherInterface } from '@orkestrel/router'\n1097:export function connectRelay(\n1100:): { readonly browser: ProviderInterface; readonly dispatcher: DispatcherInterface } {\n../scaffold/.claude/rules/names.md:6:# Naming and API-shape rules\r\n../scaffold/.claude/rules/names.md:10:## Entity-scoped names: one word\r\n../scaffold/.claude/rules/names.md:32:- Private methods: two or three words are acceptable.\r\n../scaffold/.claude/rules/names.md:36:## Split instead of compounding\r\n../scaffold/.claude/rules/names.md:40:### Group options by entity\r\n../scaffold/.claude/rules/names.md:51:### Extract sub-entities\r\n../scaffold/.claude/rules/names.md:65:### Split behavioral variants\r\n../scaffold/.claude/rules/names.md:83:## Standalone helpers\r\n../scaffold/.claude/rules/names.md:108:## General vocabulary\r\n../scaffold/.claude/rules/names.md:124:## Acronyms\r\n../scaffold/.claude/rules/names.md:136:## Type-level identifiers\r\n../scaffold/.claude/rules/names.md:155:## Value-level identifiers\r\n","exit_code":1,"status":"failed"}}
